ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u4	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Minister of National Defence, Mr Antoni Macierewicz.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u5	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! Membership! The territorial defence forces were set up 9 months ago, and at present, they are counting over 5,000. soldiers, including 4,000. soldiers of territorial military service and more than 1 000. soldiers of professional military service. They are organized today mainly in three brigades of the eastern wall with location in Białystok, Suwałki, Łomża and Hajnowce, as well as Dęblin, Biała Podlaskie, Chełma, Rzeszów, Nisko, Dębica, Jarosław and Sanoku. These are brigades bearing the names of heroes fighting for independence from the period of World War II and the period of communist occupation, General Liniarski, Dekutowski and Ciepliński. These troops, which are already organized, which I am talking about, are going through exercises that last about, over 45, 48 days a year, exercises that are fully capable of preparing them for effective primary service. At the moment we are also completing and organizing brigades in central Polish voivodships, i.e. in warmińsko-mazurskie and mazowieckie voivodships. I mean, the headquarters of these brigades and troops will be Olsztyn, Elk, Morag, Braniewo and Giżycko, when it comes to 4. Warmia-Mazury Territorial Defence Brigade; 5. The Mazowiecka Territorial Defence Brigade will be the headquarters of Ciechanów, Komorowo, Siedlce and Zegra, and in 6. Mazovian Territorial Defence Brigade - Radom, Pomiechówek, Grójec, Książnica and Płock. This does not, of course, exhaust all the locations of the Territorial Defence Forces, but points to the main places where they will be stationed. As far as the personnel composition is concerned, the average age of the soldiers of the Territorial Defence Army is 30 and 32 years of age, with nearly 30% of the soldiers counting around 25 years old, the oldest 40 years old. About 10 and 12% All the soldiers in the Territorial Defense Army are women. That's more or less the organizational state. By the end of 2019 we want to appoint 17 brigades of the Territorial Defence Army. This deadline is not at stake today. As regards the issue of cooperation with local governments in the area of the location of the brigades and the acquisition of real estate, cooperation in various projects, it is, in general, working very well. More than 240 reconnaissances were carried out to identify the locations of the territorial defence troops. The infrastructure has been reviewed, mainly military infrastructure, i.e. 114 complexes, 41 properties remaining in the resources of the Military Property Agency, 56 properties proposed by local government units and 11 properties reported by private owners. Unfortunately, these issues sometimes face various problems and problems. The biggest concern was the Starogard of Gdańsk and the Pomeranian Voivodeship, which unfortunately resulted in quite tragic consequences during the recent storm. The City Council of Starogard did not have time to form individuals, and therefore the Territorial Defence Army could not be used at a time when there was a great need for this during recent dramatic events. Unfortunately, we also have a similar situation in Radom, where it was opposed, the city authorities opposed the originally chosen location on the street. Struga. We are not able to overcome such opposition in any way, of course, because it is [[♪ The bell ♪]] voluntary, it is the will of the city authorities. We assume that good cooperation with local governments is the foundation of the development of the Territorial Defence Army, which are primarily...

In Poland, the loss of such power would mean serious perturbations and energy shortages in Poland. Ladies and gentlemen, on the other hand, power plants, especially those producing coal electricity, are currently under very strong pressure in terms of emissions. One method of eliminating this emission is cogeneration. In Rybnik there is already a little use of non-replaceable heat. We have unofficial information that PEC Hawks-Zdrój is conducting discussions with EDF on the possibility of providing heat. There is a question of building a bus line about 4 km long. It is an investment that requires certain measures. However, I stress that this heat from cogeneration is a very attractive heat, but investment in linear infrastructure, in district heating networks still needs to be done, investment in heating equipment in homes also needs to be done. The biggest financial problem in our assessment will be these endings of the network with citizens. It is easier to build in blocks, the most expensive is in individual houses, because it is a distributed network, it is relatively small to receive in relation to investment expenditure. Ladies and gentlemen, how is this alcohol distributed? First of all, it is not distributed at legal points of sale, because we as customs and treasury services regularly control these points. In particular, there is no way to compete with illegal alcohol, given the fiscal burdens that are actually involved in these excise goods. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u51	Please respond to the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Digitisation of Mr Marek Zagórski.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u52	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! One of the main objectives of the Ministry of Digitalization is to ensure that all citizens in Poland can access broadband Internet. In line with the objectives set out in the Digital Agenda for Europe and the National Wideband Plan, we want to ensure that by 2020 all households in Poland can access the Internet with a capacity of at least 30 Mbps and build demand for even faster services, so that by the same year at least 50% households have used services with a capacity of at least 100 Mbps. We are aware of the demographic and geographical characteristics of our country and make the development of modern telecommunications infrastructure slower in many areas, particularly rural ones or much slower than in urban centres. Such areas are most often, as you said, economically unattractive, but also not economically attractive to telecom operators due to the excessive cost of investment compared to expected and acceptable investment revenues. This is, moreover, a permanent problem when it comes to building infrastructure in less urban areas with lower population density, because the cost of building such infrastructure per capita is simply much higher. We therefore consider all activities related to the provision of broadband access as an important element of the country's sustainable development and the real levelling of rural opportunities. In order to counter the permanent digital exclusion of these areas, we are implementing the Digital Poland Operational Programme, in which we have allocated over PLN 4 billion to the construction of broadband networks. So far we have announced and resolved two calls for funding, which have resulted in over 130 projects covering more than 1.3 million households and almost 7,000. location of schools - as a rule, sometimes these schools are several in one location - in so-called areas. white spots accessing the high-speed internet. In the context of the areas where we intervene, it should be pointed out that more than 80% households so far designated to cover modern infrastructure with PO PC funds are located in localities up to 5 thousand. residents, therefore overall 80% This intervention is aimed at rural areas. The amount of the grant is approx. PLN 2.4 billion, which means that we still have free funding for subsidising entrepreneurs in the implementation of projects that eliminate white spots. As a result, we are working on the organisation of the next selection of applications for funding, because not all areas of Poland have been interested in investors as part of the previous two recruitments. In addition to grants under OP PCs, we also implement a support programme primarily for small and medium-sized telecoms entrepreneurs in the form of loans. For this purpose we have allocated PLN 1 billion. The operator of this project is Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. It has already identified one operator, Alior Bank, and these loans will be available to small and medium-sized telecommunication companies in a moment so that their ability to invest in broadband networks is greater. A very important element, which we consider to be a flywheel of Internet access, the development of high-speed broadband Internet, is the draft law on the construction of the National Education Network adopted by the Council of Ministers on Tuesday. As part of this project, we intend to lead all schools to the investment of telecommunications operators, but above all through the free mechanism... I mean, we want the Internet in all schools to be free. This means really funding from the state budget, not from the budgets of local government units or other bodies running schools, which we believe will increase the attractiveness of telecommunications operators' investments in broadband networks and will stimulate the development of these networks also outside schools in rural areas - mainly rural, although of course the OSE will be directed to all schools in Poland. In order to make investments in telecommunications infrastructure less capital intensive, last year we followed a law amending a package of laws that concerns the investment process in telecommunications infrastructure. We have therefore shortened this process and reduced its costs. at least at the level of administrative procedures. We also have a dialogue with local government representatives to build awareness of the need to reduce costs, for example the costs associated with the occupation of the road, because this is often a barrier to investment, especially in rural areas. Finally, it is worth pointing out that we are involved in the work on the initiative of the European Commission. Wi-Fi for you, according to which the municipalities will be able to receive EU credits for the development of points, so-called. free hotspots, in communes. We are already at such a stage that a compromise has been reached between the European Parliament and the Council on the objectives of this initiative. We look forward to specific proposals on the support system. The first call is scheduled for the end of 2018 and 2019. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u53	Thank you very much. I would like an additional question from Mr Jarosław Goncier.

This mechanism is very closely linked to what I mentioned earlier, namely the Digital Poland Operational Programme, i.e. the grants we provide for the construction of broadband infrastructure in areas of white spots, but not only. It will also be linked to the investment plans of telecommunications operators, i.e. that the telecommunications operator will build a link to the school and then the OSE operator will use it, lease it on commercial terms, so we will pay for it. Of course, when it comes to this extraction process, where there is more than one supplier, one operator in a given area, this will be a competitive procedure. Where this infrastructure exists on the basis that there is one operator, it will first use its services. We think that this will also be an impulse for telecom operators to speed up the process of connecting schools to broadband Internet, with a guarantee that schools will receive this service, because the state will, in turn, finance it from the state budget. What is important is that schools will have an obligation to provide high-speed broadband, leading authorities will have an obligation to provide this Internet in schools. They will be able to use either the free offer of the OSE operator or buy this Internet on a commercial basis. This obligation will be real since 2021. Thank you very much. [[Thank you.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u69	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Michał Wójcik.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u70	Dear Marshal, Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you so much for that question. For several months now, there has been such a lively debate on parental abductions, but also on caring matters. I would like to begin by thanking the committees that have been leaning on this issue very warmly, among others. The Committee on Communications with Poles abroad and the Committee on Petitions. We've been talking about these things. These are delicate matters. You know, we're sensitive. It's about the children's welfare. I want to say yes at first. After a dozen months of my work in the Ministry of Justice, when I was ordered to diagnose this problem and prepare solutions, today I want to inform you that we have prepared a draft of changes, or actually a draft law that has never functioned. This is the biggest paradox of this whole case that there has never been a Central Authority Act, which is the Ministry of Justice. The paradox was that for years cases that had affected the Ministry of Justice have been settled in a customary way for years, despite the fact that the background was about children's well-being and very sensitive matters, civil, criminal, international issues have arisen. We have prepared a draft law on the Central Authority, which is the Ministry of Justice, in order to define very carefully the procedure, what should happen to the proposal at a time when such a case affects our ministry. We were one of the last countries in Europe that had no such regulation. I would also like to say that, until recently, these cases - this is unbelievable - only two people were involved in the Ministry of Justice. Today we have a special department that's handling this. This is the department that operates within the department of international affairs. This department covers 17 people, but it seems that there should be further reinforcement of staff due to the increase in cases. It was just a question of how much that number increased. By 30%, the number of cases increased. Why? Because we've finally started talking about how you're gonna stand up for a citizen, that you're gonna stand up for a citizen, but of course there's always gonna be a child's welfare in the background. Because it's not always the case that a Polish citizen is right. You have to keep that in mind, too. But our citizens often, because the state didn't stand up for them, were losing to the citizens of foreign countries. We have prepared a project that assumes four basic elements. The first one is specialization. Specialized courts will be set up. It's not about more than 300 district courts, it's about 11 district courts. 11 district courts. We move these cases to a higher level and create specialist judiciary. This is not about 45 courts of appeal, it is about one court of appeal. It's not about the county courts. It's about one appeal court. A special department will be established in the Court of Appeal in Warsaw. What does that mean? This means that not a few thousand judges will deal with these matters, but 30 and 40 people, 30 and 40 trained, trained judges on a national scale. We are preparing all the trainings within the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, so that they are people equipped with knowledge. Plus, we're giving new tools to the Attorney General. This is a cassation complaint. There are extremely complex, difficult and sometimes objectionable issues at a time when the case ends with a final settlement. Until now, the Attorney General has not been able to intervene. Today he will have a cassation complaint - an emergency appeal. He'll have the right to do that. Another issue that raised doubts is the issue of children after the first instance. The decision at first instance meant that the child could have left our country the next day. We are introducing a new regulation: ban on children until the case is settled. This is very important and refers to European standards. Finally, this is also about the so-called case. Lawyer's coercion, which I have touched gently. There's our citizen. The fact is, he's assuming he's gonna win the case because he's right. The fact that he's right is one thing, but that's one thing to protect. That's why our citizens often lose because they collide with the machine, you can say, lawyers from other countries. Therefore, there will be a legal obligation - the obligation to participate in such cases. And that means that if people can't afford such a defender, then such a proxy can be assigned to her by law. And that's an important thing too. We also promote mediation. As early as October, a special seminar will be held with representatives of the German Ministry of Justice. We want most or many cases to be settled through mediation. This is an instrument that has not been used so far. Finally, I would also like to thank the prosecution, the prosecutors of Święczkowski and Hernand of the Public Prosecutor's Office and the Deputy Prosecutor General. The first thing we did was ask the prosecutors to take part in all these cases. And that's what's happening today. Polish prosecutors are involved in abduction cases. Special guidelines have been issued in this respect. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u85	Thank you very much. And please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Mr Bartosz Cichocki.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u86	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much to Mr Mieczysław Miazga, Mr Zbigniew Chmielowiec and Mr Kazimierz Gołojuch for the questions addressed to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. I'd be happy to answer that. As regards the legal basis, the legal basis is the Green Card system, which has been in operation since 1 January 1953. Its purpose is to ensure that victims of traffic accidents receive compensation for the damage caused by a driver of a vehicle registered abroad, and to avoid the obligation to purchase motor insurance at the border of each of the countries visited. Within the framework of the Green Card system, there is a multilateral agreement whereby drivers of vehicles registered in signatory countries can move within their territories without the need for a green card. The multilateral agreement includes 32 of the 47 members of the Green Card scheme. Polish citizens therefore need a green card in all those countries which are part of the Green Card system but do not sign a multilateral agreement. In other cases, normal OC is sufficient. The group of those countries which belong to the system but do not sign a multilateral agreement is the Republic of Macedonia, which at the border collects the fees mentioned by the Member and requires a green card. The green card in most insurance companies operating in Poland is issued free of charge or for a small fee. The fee that you are asking me, Members, is so-called. border insurance, which must be purchased only by those Polish drivers who do not have a green card, e.g. They didn't pick her up from their insurance company or they forgot to take her away on a foreign trip. I would like to point out that information about the need to have a green card when crossing the Macedonian border, as well as the consequences of the lack of it, i.e. the need to buy border insurance, is provided in the guide I would add on this occasion that citizens of the Republic of Macedonia are also obliged to have a green card when entering Poland. By clarifying the answer to the question of the link between this requirement and the attempt to enter the European Union - there is no such link here. States are encouraged to join the multilateral agreement on the basis of the UN Geneva Recommendation, i.e. at the level of the United Nations. The European Union does not require such a... This is not part of the accession negotiations. Of course, Poland can and will encourage facilitations for travel, mutually beneficial facilitations, including Macedonia and other countries that are in the green card system, but are not yet members of the multilateral agreement. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u92	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! As far as the first question is concerned, all food that is placed on the market in all European Union countries, including food imported from countries outside the Union, must comply with all the requirements of European Union legislation. With regard to L-cysteine, which is only an additive to flour as an enhancer, it must comply with the requirements of the European Commission Regulation According to the provisions of this Regulation, human hair cannot be used as a starting material for the production of this type of improver as a material for the production of L-cysteine. According to Art. 14 and 17 Regulations 178 of the European Parliament of 2002 laying down the general principles and requirements of food law, establishing the European Food Authority. Food safety and food safety procedures on the market must not be hazardous to human health and life. The responsibility for this safety is borne by the entrepreneur. Entrepreneurs in the food industry are subject to control at both the production and marketing stage as regards the application of these provisions of food law by the official food control authorities, including the State Sanitary Inspection, which operates on the basis of the State Sanitary Inspection Act. Direct supervision of the activities of food business operators, i.e. those responsible for the entire food production and processing process, including the supervision of bakeries, shall be exercised under this law by the authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection which control the use of the relevant substances which are used for the production, including their labelling and traceability, the conditions of use and documentation confirming that the product complies with food safety requirements. Documentation check for these additives, such as: L-cysteine primarily concerns purity criteria, which are laid down in the provisions of the European Commission Regulation recognising purity criteria for additives, including the origin of the substances from which these additives were produced. Foods which are transported from third countries to the territory of the European Union shall be placed on the market subject to a border health inspection carried out by the authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection. These food controls are also carried out in the framework of the supervision of the establishments themselves. Therefore, in addition to border controls, controls are also carried out at the level of food production and marketing establishments. So that as far as legislation is concerned, this is all quite strictly described and respected by the authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection. We were not informed that the origin of L-cysteine could be inappropriate, contrary to European Union legislation. As regards other additives, such as calcium propionate or potassium sorbitan, which, like L-cysteine, belong to a group of food additives with specific E- Potassium propionate is E282, potassium sorbitan is E202, L-cysteine is E920 - only those additives that have been authorised by the relevant provisions I mentioned earlier can be used. These additives may be used for the purposes of the so-called "supplies'. technologically, which is precisely defined by the European Union legislation mentioned just now. As far as the details of this legislation are concerned, this is a regulation The European Parliament on food additives from 2008. That Regulation constitutes general rules for the use of food additives, the list of authorised food additives and their conditions of use for certain categories of foodstuffs, including their maximum levels. The legal act which contains detailed requirements for the use of food additives, including their doses and conditions of use, is the 2011 European Commission Regulation. There all this is discussed thoroughly, and the Polish services, I think, also closely supervise compliance with these regulations. As far as these improvers are concerned, both L-cysteine and calcium propionate and potassium sorbitan, which can be used for rye bread and bread cut as preservatives, whereas L-cysteine as so-called. the improver, this use of these substances must comply with the provisions already mentioned and also justified by the technology itself. Information on their use must be given on the product itself, i.e. as the material composition from which the type of bread was produced. On the other hand, when it comes to the possibility of carrying out an information campaign on this, it is necessary, of course, to consider whether it is necessary to inform people that the bread from the classic [[♪ The bell ♪]] baking is better, even tasty, than such a deep frozen one, but it seems that these regulations, which clearly dictate the use of material composition, are sufficient. But if there's a conclusion...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u226	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the State of Israel on social security signed at Jerusalem on 22 November 2016. The Law and Justice Club supports this government submission. The agreement addresses the important problems of Polish and Israeli citizens in the field of social security. Each country has its own social security system. The problem arises when a citizen of one country works in another country for some time. In such a case, it is difficult or even impossible to preserve the rights acquired in connection with social security. Therefore, bilateral international agreements are needed to coordinate the operation of two different systems. This agreement can also contribute to the development of economic cooperation between Poland and Israel, it will create a better climate for mutual investment of Polish entrepreneurs in Israel and Israeli entrepreneurs in Poland. The double payment of social security contributions will be eliminated and will have a negative impact on economic development. The commitment to the social security agreement with the State of Israel must also be seen in the context of the Association Agreement between the European Union and Israel. On the basis of this Association Agreement, a common EU approach to the coordination of social security systems between the Member States of the European Union and the State of Israel is envisaged. This agreement between the Republic of Poland and the State of Israel is slightly ahead of the arrangements which will be adopted in a broader EU context and which will be based, as this agreement, on the principle of equal treatment of legally employed workers and the principle of the obligation to transfer benefits from social security schemes such as pensions, survivor's pensions, invalidity pensions, pensions for accidents at work and occupational diseases. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u227	Thank you, sir. And please take the floor of Mrs Ewa Kołodziej, Parliamentary Club Civic Platform. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u228	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is my great pleasure to present to the High House the position of the Civic Platform Club of the Republic of Poland on the draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the State of Israel on social security signed in Jerusalem on 22 November 2016, prints 1693 and 1754. This agreement is highly expected and very desirable because it allows for the establishment of a basis for coordination of social security systems in both countries and thus provides insurance protection for citizens of both countries. So far there have been no legal regulations between Poland and Israel in the field of social security, which has brought with it many disadvantages for the citizens of both countries. It was not possible to qualify for the benefits of periods of insurance in the territory of the other party. There were also no rules governing the transfer of benefits to persons who had moved to the territory of the other country. This was a serious problem for many Polish and Israeli families, and it had a bad impact on the image of both countries among investors. The agreement signed on 22 November 2016 in Jerusalem aims to address these problems by regulating legal issues in this area. In its content, this agreement ensures that the principles of equal treatment of transfers of funds and the aggregation of periods of employment are met. It also assumes that persons falling within its scope will be subject to the laws of only one country, indicating at the same time which State legislation is appropriate in a particular case. Contract records are organized, among others. employment, business, social security contributions, pensions, accident and occupational disease benefits, maternity benefits and funeral allowances. It is also worth mentioning that the conclusion of this agreement will probably have a positive impact on the image of both countries among entrepreneurs interested in doing business in the territory of the other party. Particularly important from this point of view is the provision on the elimination of double payment of social security contributions. In conclusion, I would like to inform the High House that the Parliamentary Club of the Civic Platform of the Republic of Poland will vote in favour of the bill, 1693 and 1754 printed today. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u236	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Health, I would like to present the draft law on specific solutions for improving the quality and accessibility of healthcare provided in print No 1820. The draft law on specific solutions for improving the quality and accessibility of healthcare services has two objectives: wider and faster access to health services for patients and a reduction of several years of queues by increasing the financial resources for medical equipment and equipment. The Act will allow the Minister responsible for Health to transfer grants to medical equipment and apparatus both to medical entities established and supervised by him and to those who provide healthcare services. Today, oncology centres are primarily among them in terms of needs. The proposed regulations result from the unsatisfactory technical state of the medical infrastructure, which requires funding and investment. A dynamically changing situation requires adapting the diagnostic and therapeutic potential to the needs of society. It also requires new medical and IT technologies. Hence the need to constantly invest and provide resources for health protection. Needless to say, medical equipment needs to be operational and in good condition. Medicine's developing. We cannot ignore the achievements of the latest medicine, and thus technological and technological progress, and this also involves the need for continuous and continuous investment. Thus, a large part of the funding is devoted to the purchase of medical equipment and equipment, with an emphasis on modernising diagnostics and therapy primarily in the fields of paediatrics and child surgery, as well as oncology, as the number of cancer patients is constantly increasing. Purchases of diagnostic equipment and minimally invasive treatment, including purchases of laparoscopes, thoracoscopics or gastroscopes, are particularly important. Modern non-invasive diagnostic equipment is also desirable, e.g. modern ultrasounds, especially for small patients. In the case of oncology, emphasis was placed on the development of brachytherapy in the project. In addition, the project provides for the purchase of equipment for emerging breast cancer treatment centres and the purchase of equipment to equip laser therapy centres in children with congenital vascular anomalies. These children are treated abroad because of the lack of available laser therapy methods in the country and the long waiting period, and this is a huge expense of tens of thousands of euros. As many voivodships are temporarily lacking in intensive care facilities for the newborn, the project envisages an increase in funding for medical equipment and equipment for healthcare providers who provide hospital care services for the neonatology units of the third reference level. It is necessary to expand the infrastructure for intensive care of the newborn, and this results, among other things. with the increasing number of newborn births with burdens, that we can save today - and that is what we do - extremely immature premature babies that require long treatment, and that we finally have more births. It should be added that medical equipment for intensive care of the newborn is largely exploited and requires replacement or addition, so its purchase for the neonatology departments of the third reference level will allow for improved neonatological care results, improve diagnosis and treatment. Another important issue is the provision of health prevention offices in schools run by a local government unit. Preventive health care for children and adolescents of school age will ensure the proper development of children and young people and, in the long run, the proper functioning of children and young people in adult life. Therefore, students must have access to a health care clinic where a nurse, possibly a hygienist, will give them advice or medical assistance. According to the Education Law and public-funded health care legislation, every school should have a health prevention office. Its creation and equipment in the case of schools run by a local government unit is a function of its own. A separate provision is the regulation of the Article. 6 the project, which provides for a 100% financing of the equipment of health prevention offices, which is necessary in view of the need to provide pupils with access to preventive health care benefits regardless of the financial capacity and priorities of the local government unit running the school. Prevention both prevents disease and significantly reduces the cost of possible treatment. The functioning of the health prevention cabinet in each school is consistent with the work being carried out on the design of the Health Act for children and young people of school age. The project also provides for strengthening the role of a nurse or a school hygienist working with a primary health care doctor and a dentist in the field of preventive care for students. The provision by the voivodship of local government units of grants intended to equip health prevention offices will enable proper preventive care of students. In addition to purchasing equipment for health prevention in schools, the project also provides for the purchase of dentobuses, i.e. vehicles in which prevention and dental assistance are to be carried out. The data contained in the program "Monitoring the health status of the oral population of the Polish population" say that among three-year-olds the percentage free of caries is 46.2% , among 6-year-olds - only 14.4%. One quarter of the parents and carers of children aged 3 and 6 did not take their child to the dentist, and one third of the parents did not care about the oral hygiene of their children's mouth or control it. And that's a problem that's very important. These school offices also need to be helped. It doesn't look better in older age groups. The data are so alarming that effective measures must be taken to promote health and include systematic dental prophylaxis for children of school age. Offices and dentobuses will help to prevent and provide dental care to students of families in difficult material situations. The project foresees that dentobuses purchased by the Minister responsible for Health will be transferred to voivodeships who will make them available free of charge to healthcare providers with whom the NFZ has concluded a dental care contract for dental treatment performed in the dentobus. The provider would have the cost of maintaining the dentobus. After the end of the contract with the National Health Fund, the provider would return the dentobus to the voivodeship. The cost of dental health benefits will be borne from 2018 under the funding provided by the National Health Fund's financial plan. The project deals with the purchase of vaccines to carry out compulsory preventive vaccinations in accordance with the preventive vaccination programme established for the year concerned and therefore increases the limit of the maximum expenditure for 2017 and 2018 borne by the State budget. The need to increase these limits in 2017 is linked inter alia. with the entry into force of the Regulation of the Minister of Health of 3 November 2016, which introduced an obligation to vaccinate all children born after 31 December 2016 against pneumococci. In line with the public procurement rules and the need for a tendering procedure, a purchase should be made in 2017. The maximum limit of the abovementioned expenditure for 2018 has been adjusted to the amount of the financial resources provided for this purpose in the draft budget law for 2018. The amendment of these limits does not have a direct impact on the State budget. The amendment is intended to allow the possible purchase of pneumococcal vaccines in 2017 and in 2018 to adapt the limit to the appropriations planned for this task in the draft budget law for 2018. A short period of time for the implementation of the tasks and the need to finance them by the end of 2017, as well as the deadlines indicated in the draft for requesting funds from the special-purpose reserve, are due to the real possibility of developing appropriate proposals. The urgent entry into force of the Act on the day following the date of its publication is the subject of an important interest of the State. The draft shall not require the competent institutions and bodies of the European Union, including the European Central Bank, to be notified, consulted or agreed upon in order to obtain an opinion. The project is compatible with European Union law. The project will affect medium, small and micro-entrepreneurs. This impact will result from the conclusion of agreements with some of them with voivodships on the availability of dentobuses. The draft does not contain technical provisions within the meaning of the Council of Ministers Regulation of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts and is therefore not subject to the notification procedure. All Poles have been waiting for years for the changes introduced by the above law. Simple rule: prevention is better than cure. Healthy Hansel will grow into a healthy John. For many years with doctors, authorities in the world of medicine, I promoted together with people of passion, and promoted prevention, and now our dreams and all Poles are coming true, because healthy children are a healthy society and consequently a healthy economy. For almost 2 years, the good direction of government action on behalf of Law and Justice, which is concerned about the health and dignity of Poles, is really investing in the future of Poland. High Izbo! Simple rule: we're not supposed to fight against it, we're supposed to fight for society. Please, over political divisions together, let us take care of the health of Poles. This is a big deal. Thank you. [[Applause]]

I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Piotr Nowak to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u284	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The draft law presented to the High House is the implementation by the Republic of Poland of the obligations laid down in Regulation No 1286/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 November 2014 on key information documents for retail collective investment products and insurance investment products, commonly referred to as PRIIP, which will be applied directly from 1 January 2018. Currently, the information provided to individual investors wishing to conclude investment agreements, e.g. insurance contract with an insurance capital fund, are uncoordinated and often do not help to compare different products or understand their investment characteristics, i.e. the risks associated with investing in such products. The consequence is that investors often invest without understanding the risks and costs involved, thus resulting in unforeseen losses. The EU PRIIP Regulation requires financial market institutions to produce key product information documents, so-called. KIDs, and hand them over to customers before the contract is concluded. The regulation ensures that a common standard is established for the creation of such documents, allowing for harmonisation of their format and content, which will make it easier to compare certain types of products. In accordance with the provisions of the PRIIP Regulation, a key product information document should be formulated in an accurate, reliable, clear and non-misleading manner. In order to ensure comparability, it should be drawn up on no more than three pages in format A4 in print form. The transmission of a standardised document will provide the customer with relevant information including, inter alia,: identification and contact details of the manufacturer of the product, identification of the nature and main characteristics of the product, brief description of the risk profile and potential benefits, presentation of the investment costs, both one-off and repeated, identification of the recommended minimum duration of the contract and the possibility to lodge a complaint. It also empowers designated authorities to monitor the market for insurance investment products and to intervene in relation to a product that poses a risk to investor protection, the proper functioning of the market or the stability of the entire financial system. Such intervention will result in a ban or restriction on the distribution and sale of the product concerned. Although the EU regulation will apply directly, it is necessary to adopt provisions in Polish law allowing the application of EU regulations. The draft law amending the Law on Financial Market Supervision and the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities provides for the designation of the Commission for Financial Supervision as the body responsible for supervision, compliance and enforcement of the Regulation and the imposition of administrative penalties for non-compliance with Union legislation. In addition, the draft law provides for the implementation of the national option from the article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the PRIIP Regulation by requiring the insurance undertaking to provide the Financial Supervision Commission with a key information document at least 30 days before the start of the proposal and at least 14 days before the start of the proposal to update the functioning document. Both the draft law and this regulation will significantly improve the position and safety of consumers with investment products contracts by providing them with certain information in a standardised form prior to the conclusion of the contract. In turn, the mechanism for monitoring the market for insurance investment products and intervention against those products causing the risk should ensure effective elimination of products presenting a risk to customers and the proper functioning of the market so as not to repeat the so-called "market." a common problem with polysolocats. In view of the need to increase the protection of individual investors and the urgent need to ensure the functioning of European Union law, I very much ask the honourable House to work constructively and urgently on the draft law submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u285	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Maria Zuba, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u286	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Today we begin work on a government bill amending the Law on Financial Market Supervision and the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities. The project contains legal regulations implementing European Union legislation. Their introduction will increase the level of customer protection. The amendment of two laws, the Financial Market Surveillance Act and the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities proposed by the Government transfers the provisions contained in the European Union Regulation to the Polish legal order, so it is a kind of instruction setting out detailed requirements for the creation of documents containing key information on retail collective investment products and insurance investment products. The requirements laid down in this project will apply to product makers and sellers. According to the provision in Article. 4 point 8 of the Union Regulation competent authorities to supervise the implementation of the requirements imposed by this Regulation on the creators of retail collective investment products and insurance investment products and the persons advising or selling those products shall mean the national authorities designated by the Member State. In this project, the government points out that in Poland the authority responsible for supervision, compliance and enforcement of the provisions of Regulation No 1286/2014 of the European Union is the Financial Supervision Commission. It shall entrust the committee with the imposition of administrative penalties in accordance with national law. The provisions of the Act clearly specify the scope of these powers and the maximum amount of penalties imposed. The EU rules set penalties for a legal entity up to EUR 5 million and for a natural person up to EUR 7 000. Euros. At the same time, they specify that in Member States whose currency is not the euro, the highest penalty is the equivalent of that amount converted in national currency at the rate applicable on 30 December 2014. Calculated on the basis of this conversion factor and the accounting rules in force in Poland, the designers shall determine the upper limit of the penalty in the provision of the article. Article 3c (3) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the Before deciding on the application of the sanctions, the committee shall take into account all the relevant circumstances which are indicated in the provision of the Article. 25 of the Union Regulation and form a catalogue of areas which must be subject to prior examination and assessment, in particular as regards the seriousness of the infringement and the duration of the infringement, the degree of responsibility of the person responsible for the infringement, the impact of the infringement on the interests of individual investors, the willingness to cooperate with the person responsible for the infringement and any previous infringements committed by the person responsible for the infringement. The amounts collected for fines will constitute revenue to the State budget. The rules proposed by the designers also govern the rules on penalties to be applied to a legal person or an organisational unit without legal personality, which is either a parent undertaking or a subsidiary of a parent undertaking. The draft provides that the decision constituting [[♪ The bell ♪]] the content of the final decision shall be made public by the committee. The Law and Justice Club requests further work on the law and referrals it to the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u289	Thank you very much. Mr Grzegorz Długi, Kukiz Poselski Club15 has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u290	Without the Kukiz. Kukiz is sick. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Allow me to quote some comments on the proposal for a new law, which is, as we know, the implementation of the 2014 European Parliament and Council Regulation, as amended in 2016 by the relevant regulation, which requires, first of all, the identification of the authority that will supervise and enforce the provisions of the Regulation, including the imposition of penalties, as my predecessor has already mentioned here. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u304	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to present to the High House a new comprehensive regulation on insurance distribution. The adoption of the new Insurance Distribution Act, in place of the existing Act of 22 May 2003 on insurance intermediation, results primarily from the need to implement into the national legal order the provisions of Directive (EU) 2016/97 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 January 2016 on insurance distribution, so-called. IDD. Member States of the European Union should implement this Directive by 23 February 2018. The draft law introduces solutions to improve existing insurance mediation regulations and extend the scope of the rules to all insurance distribution channels. The aim of the bill is also to ensure undistorted competition, consumer protection and market integration. According to the IDD, customers seeking insurance protection should benefit from protection at the same level, regardless of the differences between distribution channels. The draft law follows the IDD Directive therefore covers all insurance distribution entities, irrespective of the distribution channel. Insurance distributors will therefore be both insurance intermediaries and insurance undertakings. In the case of insurance intermediaries, it is envisaged that the entity divides intermediaries into brokers and insurance agents, subject to the inadmissibility of combining the two functions. An entity's breakdown is a classic picture of insurance mediation where only brokers or agents registered as brokers can perform mediation activities. In addition, it is proposed that agency activities and agency activities, in addition to insurance agents, should also carry out a new category of entities - additional insurance agents. In turn, the direct performance by the insurance undertaking of the activities relating to the distribution of insurance will be possible through authorised employees' insurance undertakings. Such activities are defined as the distribution activities of the insurance undertaking. Notwithstanding insurance distribution, the draft law regulates the reinsurance distribution process, which has so far been regulated as part of insurance mediation, as well as the distribution process of insurance guarantee contracts. The inclusion of the provisions of the Act on the conclusion and execution of insurance guarantee contracts will ensure that the interests of clients of insurance undertakings containing such contracts and beneficiaries of those guarantees are properly secured. The IDD Directive introduces a number of additional solutions of a pro-consumer nature. The primary obligation is to ensure that insurance distributors behave honestly, fairly and professionally in accordance with the best interests of customers. Nor will the remuneration of the insurance distributor and the persons using which the agency activities will be carried out be incompatible with the obligation to act in accordance with the best interests of the customers. In the pre-contractual stage, in order to enable the customer to make an informed decision, the draft law provides for an obligation to determine, on the basis of the information obtained, its individual requirements and needs. The customer will then be provided in an understandable form with objective information about the insurance contract. In the case of property insurance, this information will be provided in the form of a standardised contract information document covering inter alia. a brief description of insurance protection, the method of payment of contributions, the obligations of the parties at different stages of the contract, the obligations in the event of a claim and the way in which the contract is terminated. Notwithstanding the provision of information about the insurance contract to the customer before the contract is concluded, information about the insurance distributor will be provided, including, but not limited to, information about the insurance distributor. information on the nature of the remuneration received in connection with the proposed conclusion of the insurance contract or insurance guarantee contract. In the case of investment insurance contracts, it is proposed to extend information obligations, inter alia. disclosure of distribution and commission costs. These costs will be disclosed in the form of indicators representing the share of the assumed acquisition costs and commission costs in the sum of the insurance premiums due under the insurance contract in the recommended minimum duration of the contract. The obligation of insurance distributors to provide customers with information on the amount of distribution costs and commissions from investment insurance contracts in order to prevent the phenomenon of so-called. Misselling, which is the wrong sale, which we also talked about on the occasion of the previous bill. This information will provide a comprehensive picture of the costs of the investment insurance contract for the seeker. Pro-consumer solutions include:: providing any information to customers free of charge, in a clear, accurate and comprehensible manner and in the official language of the Member State of the European Union in which the insurance contract is concluded, or in another language to which the parties agree; ensuring the functioning of out-of-court complaint and compensation procedures to settle disputes between customers and insurance distributors, including. on the basis of the Polish legal system, the Law on the handling of complaints by financial market entities and about the Financial Ombudsman; the obligation for insurance intermediaries and employees of insurance undertakings to receive 15 hours of professional training per year in order to provide them with appropriate professional qualifications. In view of past irregularities in the draft law, particular attention shall be paid to the distribution of investment insurance. Therefore, additional requirements are introduced in the draft law, inter alia. as regards the prevention of conflicts of interest, cross-selling, assessment of suitability and appropriateness, as well as information provided to customers. The proposed solutions are primarily designed to increase the transparency of sales of such products. The proposed solutions also involve additional tasks of the Financial Supervision Commission as a supervisory authority. The bill assumes, among others, keeping a register of insurance intermediaries in the online registration system, extending the scope of the data collected therein and monitoring the market for insurance products that are placed on the market, distributed or sold. The scope of the supervisory authority's powers will also include the application of the administrative sanctions provided for in case of failure to comply with the obligations laid down in the Act. The supervisory authority will be authorised, inter alia. to issue a public statement indicating the distributor responsible and the nature of the infringement, the imposition of a penalty or the deletion of an infringement insurance intermediary from the register. It is proposed that the Act should enter into force on 23 February 2018, i.e. with the last possible date of implementation of the provisions necessary for the implementation of the IDD Directive. Earlier, i.e. provisions ensuring the implementation of the Article should enter into force on 3 January 2018. 91 Directive 2014/65/EU on markets in financial instruments and amending Directive 2002/92/EC and Directive 2011/61/EU relating to additional customer protection requirements for investment products based on insurance. In view of the need to improve the situation of customers with insurance contracts, as well as the forthcoming deadline for implementing European Union law, I ask the High House to work constructively and urgently on the draft law on insurance distribution. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u305	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Gabriela Masłowska, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u306	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft Law on the distribution of insurance from printing No 1781 introduces new arrangements concerning the rules for the pursuit of the business of distribution of personal and property insurance and reinsurance as a result of the need to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council, which was just mentioned, of 20 January 2016. The aim of the solutions introduced, the main objective, is to increase the protection of customers using different forms of insurance, which is totally justified, because the last months and even years have provided us with quite a number of examples of unreliability, even fraud, and the resulting harm of many people, many entities. The victims were often especially elderly, less market-based and financial market-based. Although some provisions implemented by the Directive already exist in the Polish legal order, new solutions offer an opportunity to improve this situation. The Directive applies to all insurance distributors, but also provides for the subjective exemptions of certain businesses, with precise conditions to be met in order to be excluded from the provisions of that Directive. It is rational to maintain the division of insurance mediation activities between insurance brokers working in favour of insurance cover waiters and insurance agents performing activities on behalf of or for the benefit of an insurance undertaking. The specific definition of distribution activities carried out by natural and legal persons also clarifies the obligations as the substance of these activities. At the same time, the distribution of insurance and distribution of reinsurance, as well as the insurance broker and the reinsurance broker and their obligations are distinguished, which is a novelty in relation to Polish law, where the reinsurance broker was a sub-type of insurance broker. The Directive imposes information obligations on insurance and reinsurance distributors in the field of advice, a fair assessment of pre-contractual offers, which will allow for a more rational or optimal choice of the offer by the interested party, with which so far there have been serious problems. The Act clarifies what is meant by insurance and reinsurance distribution and what is not (e.g. the professional handling of claims against insurance undertakings or the liquidation and valuation of damages). It is also beneficial for customers to include insurance guarantee contracts under the Act, as well as the possibility to conclude insurance guarantee contracts via websites and to supervise them by KNF. So far, reliability, integrity and professionalism in the service of the insured customer have been required in Poland. This is also stressed in the Directive, but in addition the Directive introduces an obligation to disclose the remuneration of intermediaries, the amount of the fee paid by the customer or the commission covered by the insurance contribution, which can counter certain conflicts of interest. Also e.g. whether the distributor is working for one establishment or for several, it can also help improve customer service and help them to make a rational decision. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u319	Thank you. Ms Genowefa Tokarska, Polish People's Party, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u320	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour to present my position towards the government draft law on insurance distribution, print no. 1781. The bill, another one, is the implementation in Polish law of the European Parliament directive on insurance distribution, as well as a partial implementation of the directives on markets and financial instruments and on insurance mediation. These Directives introduce a single legal regime for the insurance market in all the Member States of the European Union, or otherwise, covering all the countries of the European Economic Area. They therefore leave, in principle, a small margin to regulate legally through individual Member States. That is why the bill contains provisions allowing cross-border insurance services. The main objective of the project is to ensure consumer protection, fair competition and integration of the insurance market. The project strongly strengthens the position of supervisors, i.e. The Financial Supervision Commission, and it sets out very detailed rules for the functioning of insurance companies, agents, brokers of insurance mediation. The project strictly defines the entities that are subject to the Act, contains an extensive vocabulary of concepts, determines the nature of the distribution of insurance, stresses that the insurance distributor is obliged to act honestly, fairly, professionally, according to the best interests of customers. The Act obliges very widely understood customer information activities, in relation to supervisory authorities, including the Financial Supervision Commission and the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority. The Act sets out organisational requirements for the entire insurance market and standards for the knowledge and skills of persons working in the system and imposes obligations for the continuous training of persons employed there. In addition, the project standardises the rules for the creation, approval and sale of insurance products, as well as the keeping of certain registers concerning both products and insurance companies. Separate emphasis is placed on insurance investment products. In the Polish legal system such a product is life insurance related to the insurance capital fund. The implementing Directive and the draft law lay down additional requirements for insurance investment products in the prevention of conflicts of interest. The draft also provides for certain sanctions, including criminal sanctions, in the event of a breach by firms of obligations and requirements arising from the submitted project. This document is quite extensive, extensive, and unfortunately contains records repeated in print project 1782, which, I think, should be eliminated in the course of further work on this project. In addition, the 10 regulations of the Minister for Development and Finance are an integral part of it, which clarify some of the solutions designed by the Act. The project has been consulted with many institutions and contains many solutions proposed by these institutions. In general, it should be assessed that the bill is a pro-consumer project that raises the awareness and safety of insured persons through the included requirements, mainly information, but also organisational and supervisory. May I just add that the draft insurance distribution law replaces the existing insurance mediation law in its entirety and amends seven other laws as well. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of passing the law on to further work in the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The newly constructed regulations that we are currently working on are designed to increase the safety of citizens using the services offered in the insurance market. Moreover, through the development of public awareness, they should also increase the interest in such products and the development of the entire industry. The tools proposed by the designers to support the fulfilment of the basic objective of the Act are mainly based on the tightening of the current conditions that insurance sellers will have to fulfil. According to the new rules, insurance distributors will be required to provide additional vocational training each year, which will increase their competence and ability to match the customer's offers. Introducing an obligation to inform customers whether a distributor is acting for one or more insurers, of the nature of their remuneration, the amount of distribution costs and commission costs, will increase the trust of the parties to the insurance industry, and will eliminate situations in which instead of the client's well-being they will be guided by their own profits. Among the solutions that benefit customers and increase their protection in this project are also among others. the decision in favour of the complaint, if the insurance agent fails to reply to it within 30 days of the submission, to include the insurance distribution market in the supervision of the KNF with the possibility of imposing administrative penalties and to give the KNF the competence to keep a register of insurance intermediaries. In summary, the regulations contained in the bill are thought out and will positively affect the entire industry. Despite some opinions that only one insurance company will benefit from their introduction, I believe that they will ultimately increase the transparency and fairness of transactions in the insurance market. I have no doubt that, above all, the citizen will gain from increasing transparency and eliminating unfair competition. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u332	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am very pleased to present to you a draft law amending the Act on Social Cooperatives and some other laws from print no. 1779. I would like to remind you that the Social Cooperatives Act was passed in April 2006, entered into force in July 2006 and for the 10 years that have passed, it has been well placed in Polish reality, especially in terms of social, professional rehabilitation, social inclusion, support for people at risk of social exclusion, combining business activity with social activities. The high interest in social cooperatives is best indicated by how rapidly the number of cooperatives that were formed has grown. In 2009, after 3 years of operation of the law, we had 187 cooperatives. Now, after 10 years, there are over 1600. Although social cooperatives are a difficult form of activity, although the founders of these cooperatives are people at risk of social exclusion, they have grown permanently into landscapes of especially local communities in our country. The aim of the proposed regulation is to facilitate the establishment and operation of social cooperatives. In view of the changes in the labour market, the economic situation of our country and the decline in unemployment, we believe that the founders of cooperatives and workers of cooperatives should be the most disadvantaged groups in the labour market, people with disabilities, people who are different from the labour market, and at the same time we believe that active social policies need to facilitate their return to society. The proposed changes have been prepared together with the non-governmental sector, with the Council of Public Use Activities. We also had a meeting in 2016, on the 10th anniversary of the bill, within the national forum of social cooperatives, where the assumptions of this project were widely discussed. We want the law that we are presenting to you to enter into force 3 months after the date of the announcement, and I hope that we will be able to create a framework for the functioning of social cooperatives again this year. As far as the changes we are planning are concerned, there are a lot of them because, as I mentioned, cooperatives had a number of limitations. 10 years ago, when the Social Cooperatives Act was passed, there were great fears about using this instrument for non-social purposes. Hence the different types of tightening and requirements, which often for people who should function in cooperatives, were unsurpassable. Therefore, the first change we propose is to extend the catalogue of people who can set up social cooperatives and join them as a member or a worker for social cooperatives. The second change, which is important for cooperatives, is to strengthen the role of workers of social cooperatives. Social cooperatives, on the one hand, have founders, members of cooperatives, but the cooperative is also pro-employment and, of course, the employment of people at risk of social exclusion is very important. We want employees of social cooperatives to also be involved in the process of social and professional reintegration, involved in decision-making, consultation processes, so that they can benefit from a number of rights which have so far been reserved for members of the cooperative. The third change we are introducing is to facilitate the establishment of social cooperatives. On the one hand, we reduce the number of founders from five to three, on the other hand we impose an obligation on the cooperative so that the remaining membership is completed within 12 months. On the one hand, there are fewer founders, but on the other hand, after a year of operation this number will again amount to five people, five members. We believe that for many of those we care most about, taking the risk of setting up a cooperative is too much effort, they need time to see that they are able to function, that they are able to run a social cooperative. One of the problems that reported to us in the social cooperative environment was the illustration. We want those social cooperatives which have been financed, co-financed by public funds, to be obliged to undergo the first sample within 6 months of the date of approval of the financial statements. The next change we are presenting to you is the possibility of creating a cooperative consortium. In order to strengthen the movement of social cooperatives, we want to enable cooperatives to create cooperative consortia in the form of an agreement. Regulation is important to increase the economic potential of cooperatives, but also to organise joint networks of production, trade, services, to organise joint promotion of cooperative activities, common participation in public procurement. We hope that this initiative will be used by cooperatives in order to be more powerful in the services market, including in the market for the provision of services to public entities. Cooperatives have often complained that contributions to the National Cooperative Council are too heavy a burden for them because, at the same time, the social cooperatives' revision unions were created and this contribution was often twice paid. We want social cooperatives to be exempted from contributions to the National Cooperative Council, which belong to the relevant audit relationship and already bear similar membership fees. Another facilitation in the functioning of social cooperatives is the proposed new instruments for support from the Labour Fund and the State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities. We want to extend these support instruments, at the same time as I said earlier, by extending the activities of cooperatives, by including new categories of socially excluded people, focusing not only on the unemployed, but also on people with disabilities and people excluded from the labour market for various reasons. One of the things that is related to the programme is to make it easier for participants in the workshops of occupational therapy, young people with disabilities to prepare for the functioning of the labour market. We want the workshop participants to be able to practice in social cooperatives, which will allow them to see whether, in fact, their work, their work activity is to be carried out, attractive to them, whether they find themselves and carry out in this type of activity. These are probably the most important changes from the point of view of the cooperative. I would very much like to ask you to support the bill and hand it over to further legislative work in the Sejm. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Social economics, including social cooperatives, are economic operators who compete in the open labour market and, at the same time, have something very important, because it is a social mission. When the first act on social cooperatives was created, when the first social cooperatives started to be formed, I remember such critical voices that flowed and from some politicians and from entrepreneurs, that these entities would primarily spoil the local market, that they would threaten local competition. Now, years later, we know that this has not happened, and nothing has happened, that cooperatives fill a very important gap in local markets that they do not want to fill with other companies, and yet they do a lot of good: they give jobs to disabled people, women who return after long breaks, maternity leave, give jobs to homeless people with low qualifications. The profits are allocated to various local and social initiatives, to the development of their employees. People who have been given the opportunity and created the conditions to work, can work, pay taxes, are very active, can run their own businesses. Thanks to social cooperatives, social economic operators cease to be at a cost and, finally, they are a very good investment. In addition, the mandatory name of the social cooperative was relaxed. Indeed, from a marketing point of view, i.e. that the cooperative was to operate on the market and be competitive, it was quite disturbing. As a result of this change, a member or employee who has set up another social cooperative or has joined such a cooperative or has entered into any activity, employment, will not have to leave the existing cooperative in the event of taking up this additional job. Of course, we welcome this because it comes out, I would say, against the rational expectations of those who function in these cooperatives. In addition, the project introduces changes to improve the transparency of management, including the obligation to establish such specific communication on the board line - staff. This, of course, is not a cure for problems, as experience with other such bodies shows, but will positively affect the identification of workers with the objectives and situation of cooperatives. We also welcome the possibility in these provisions of creating a cooperative consortium in the form of an agreement - it is, of course, functioning there, but [[♪ The bell ♪]] the legal basis is important - the possibility to reimburse insurance contributions or to make the rules on the distribution of the surplus more flexible. These are things that are definitely going in the right direction...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u361	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Social Cooperatives Act and some other laws. Social cooperatives are one of the key players in the social economy. They are an important instrument of social policy in the field of activity and social inclusion. The revised rules aim to increase the sustainability of social cooperatives by making their operations in the first phase more flexible and more efficient. Clarification of the area of activity of social cooperatives will affect the ability to fully achieve the fundamental objective of the cooperative, namely social and professional reintegration of people at risk of social exclusion, not only of members of the cooperative, but also of non-members. The amended bill extends the catalogue of people who can set up social cooperatives and join them as a member or person who provides work for cooperatives, looking for jobs that do not remain in employment or who do not have a job as carers of persons with disabilities. It is a great solution to increase the employability of people who are particularly difficult to find work taking into account their limited time opportunities. The condition which allows for the setting up of cooperatives by persons outside the catalogue is among others. the implementation of social assistance tasks and services, support for people with disabilities, the running of kindergartens and other forms of pre-school education. The new objective of social cooperatives will be to meet the needs of the population in accessing socially useful services. The bill provides for the facilitation of the establishment of a social cooperative by reducing the number of persons required to establish it from five to three, under the condition that it is mandatory for members to be included, within 12 months of the date of entry into the National Court Register, of at least two persons at risk of social exclusion. The proposed solution will eliminate barriers when completing the founding composition. If a cooperative is established by people with moderate or significant disability, the limit for persons outside the catalogue of people at risk of social exclusion will not be 50%. , but 70%. The proposed changes make it more flexible for people not at risk of social exclusion to acquire social cooperatives by eliminating the need for specific qualifications. At the same time, the draft law allows the reimbursement of social security contributions and part of the costs incurred by the employer for members, as well as for employees of non-member cooperatives. The regulations introduced will facilitate the establishment of social cooperatives, which are an important instrument of active social policy aimed primarily at professional activation.
