ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u365	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate of the Republic of Poland at its meeting on 15 July 2017 examined the Act amending the Environmental Law Act and adopted three amendments to this Act. By adopting Amendment 1, the Senate considered it reasonable to grant environmental and water management funds, hereinafter referred to as the voivodship funds, at the statutory level, the competence to determine the organisation of the internal offices of the voivodship funds, so that there is no doubt as to the competence to regulate the above mentioned. issues as well as the body to which they are entitled. In addition, the amendment introduces a mandate for the Minister responsible for the environment to define by regulation the detailed mode of action of the regional authorities of the funds and the way in which powers are given in order to harmonise these regulations and to ensure the smooth operation and proper performance of the voivodship funds, bearing in mind that in practice these issues have been regulated differently in the statutes of the various voivodship funds. The proposed amendment will also lead to the absence of the need to adopt statutes by the voivodship funds. Amendment 3 aims to maintain the statutes of the voivodship funds allocated by the voivodships until the entry into force of the above-mentioned Regulation of the Minister for the Environment. At the same time, a provision was proposed allowing the Minister to issue this regulation within one month of the entry into force of the amendment. Article 4 is replaced by the following: The proposed amendment 2 seeks to regulate the situation in which, until the entry into force of the law, the Regional Supervisory Board of the Fund has failed to fulfil its obligation under Article. 3 (1) (a) 4 of the Act of 7 April 2017 amending the Act on Environmental Protection, i.e. within 14 days of its appointment, it did not address a request to the Management Board of the voivodship for the appointment of members of the Management Board of the voivodship fund. The proposed regulation provides for the voivodeship to be given competence to submit proposals for the appointment of members of the management board of the voivodship fund to the management board of the voivodship agreed with the minister responsible for the environment within 7 days of the entry into force of the Act. If in such a situation the management board of the voivodship does not appoint a member of the management board of the voivodship fund, the appointment shall be carried out by the voivodship. The amendment also regulates the consequences of the failure by the voivodship board to make available Article. 3 (1) (a) The Board of Directors shall have the power to appoint the members of the Management Board. In order to avoid doubting the consequences of the appointment of members of the Management Board of the Regional Fund in the above mentioned. the way it is proposed to add a regulation whereby these appointments are equivalent to the expiry of the mandate of the members of the existing Management Board. The same rule will apply to the vocations made pursuant to Article. 400 lips. 2a Act Environmental Law until the date of entry into force of the Act under consideration. At the same time, a provision was introduced to clarify clearly the effect of the expiry of the mandates of the current members of the Management Board of the Regional Fund from the point of view of the Labour Code. Ladies and gentlemen, Marshal of the Sejm, in accordance with Art. 54 (1) (a) 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the Senate's resolution on the above matter to the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry. The Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, after considering this resolution at its meeting on 13 September 2017, recommends that the High House adopt the three amendments tabled by the Senate during the vote. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u376	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Excuse me for making you take a break. I am already answering the question about the state of implementation of the Special Economic Zones Act. I will report on the last day of last year, 31 December 2016. This information, which I would like to convey to you, covers issues such as the location and level of development of each of the 14 zones, the effects of these zones taking into account the number of permits granted, the jobs created, the value and the industry structure of the investments made and the country of origin of capital, as well as information on the activities of companies managing zones. Last year, 12 of the 14 zones were affected by changes in the boundaries of the zones. The total area has increased by more than 1.5 thousand. ha, i.e. To the level of almost 21.5 thousand. ha. In an area of more than 500 ha 41 investment projects will be located, where the average level of zoning in total, whole areas, amounted to almost 60% and ranged from 26% in the Legnica Special Economic Zone to over 74% in the Łódź Special Economic Zone. At the end of last year, entrepreneurs had over 2200 valid permits to operate in the zones, and in 2016 257 permits were issued: from the smallest, from three - in the Kamienna Góra and Starachowice zone, to 40 - in the Mielec zone. Compared to the previous year, the number of licences issued increased by 18%. The accumulated value of invested capital since the beginning of the zones amounted to nearly PLN 112 billion, i.e. growth by more than 570 million compared to 2015, i.e. an increase of 0.5%. In 2016, in terms of the cumulative value of the investment, the largest one was the Wałbrzych zone - more than 21% whole, Łódź - 14% , Katowice - more than 12% , the smallest, but still the scored growth - the pole zone. Investors in all these zones employed over 300,000. people, from 4,000. in the post zone up to almost 60,000. in the Katowice zone. Compared to the previous year, employment increased by more than 6%. That's over 20,000. jobs. As regards the structure of the investment according to the country of origin - it is a question of participation in the total capital - it is so that almost 21% are Polish, foreign companies - more than 80%. Germany remains a foreign leader, followed by the Netherlands, the United States, Belgium and Italy. The industrial structure of the investment has also not changed significantly compared to the previous year. The largest share of accumulated investments was by companies from the automotive industry. Next are rubber products, plastics and finally metal products. According to data provided by the Ministry of Finance, the total amount of public aid granted to enterprises operating in zones by the end of 2015 amounted to just over PLN 19 billion. This represents around 17% the investment expenditure incurred by entrepreneurs by the end of 2015 and this balance remains favourable to the state budget. In 2016, little more than PLN 300 million was spent on the construction of infrastructure in the area of zones, and the expenditure of management companies amounted to almost PLN 118 million. The remainder of the expenditure was borne by municipalities, media gestors and the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways. The total amount of tax exemption of companies managing zones since the start of operation amounted to almost PLN 260 million and in 2016 alone. - almost 18 million and was higher by 4 million compared to the previous one, 2015. The highest profit was achieved by the Katowice Special Economic Zone, the Industrial Development Agency Branch in Tarnobrzeg - it is worth recalling that two zones are managed by the Industrial Development Agency and Tarnobrzeska is one of them - and Suwałska Special Economic Zone. And that's a very positive surprise when it comes to the last entity that hasn't recorded such profits in years. These profits are hesitant... In the case of the Katowice zone it is over 20 million, in the case of Suwałska - up to 7.5 million. None of the companies recorded a negative financial result. And this is also a positive, positive change from last year, where in the case of three zones we had a negative financial result. Zone management companies are involved in the creation of new clusters, participate in the work supporting cooperation within existing clusters. Last year, for example. in the Katowice zone began the process of dual studies. The Tarnobrzeg Special Zone has been a member of the automotive cluster since January and a patronage class was launched in Wałbrzych in the Centre for Vocational and Continuing Training in Shooting. So, in conclusion, last year was very good for the zones. First of all, last year the enlargement processes started in the worst case 3 years earlier were completed. They successfully answered the questions to investors, so that they could be allowed to operate in the zone. As far as positive developments are concerned, which is worth noting, the process of examining applications has been shortened since on average, as we have analysed, it lasted 24 months. This time has been reduced to 6 months. What is more, it has been launched, in parallel to the possibility of observing the legislative process, how it is progressing, that it is transparent, so that you can see at what stage the legislative process is at... Similarly, everyone can also see at the moment where, at which stage the process of granting zone permits is being examined. As I say, the duration of this process has been reduced to six months. This is related to the policy implemented in the Ministry of Development in a transparent manner, with the implementation of the so-called model. one-stop shop. Investors who are interested in investing in Poland, not only in special economic zones, get rapid information in this one-stop shop and then are carried out by the process very quickly, if of course it is complete and complete documentation. If she's not here, they're getting quick feedback. So that, as I am saying, the duration of this process has been reduced to 6 months, hence these results, both in terms of enlargement, planned investment value and planned increased number of jobs. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u384	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Information refers to the year 2016, and this is the time in which special economic zones... This is a multi-month process of promoting areas, tedious negotiations, creating infrastructure conditions. One can say that this report praises the predecessors. After all, the investment in the special zone and beyond is prepared in cooperation with the Polish Investment and Trade Agency. This often means two to three years of negotiations. Let us now ask the fundamental question: Are special economic zones needed, or are they for the economic development of the regions? Without tax breaks and other support, e.g. Armed land, potential investors would be interested in our country to this extent? There is no doubt that zones are jobs, investments, new technologies, work for around-zone companies, e.g. construction, suppliers, etc. Surely the zones are a stimulator, even a locomotive, in the development of the regions and should be supported. You just have to ask yourself if you want to be sure at all costs. Consider another aspect. Since the State Treasury is the majority or privileged owner of the zones, it is often the case that the zones are more concerned with its interests than with the interests of the local authorities where the investment is located. In this context, it is often the case that the local government is exerting all its power to provide the necessary technical infrastructure and, unfortunately, the exemption granted by it from the property tax is being sucked out until the state aid is exhausted, even though the company is already paying CIT tax to the tax authorities. According to local authorities, it should be that local taxes are paid first. Such a solution would allow them to recover the cost of their investments. At regional congresses, local government officials speak loudly about the need to replace the shares of the State Treasury in the zones with shares of voivodships. At that time, both investment geography and public aid components would be much more regionally oriented. It's time to ask what the future of the zones will be. Today's legislation states that the zones will operate in their current form and in accordance with the existing rules until the end of 2026, and for a serious business it is not a very long time. This may therefore result in less interest of potential investors. It will certainly be necessary to consider the possibility of a further extension in the near future, as investors receive public aid in the form of tax exemptions. First they have to build, start a company, have profits, and then enjoy the benefits. 2026 may not be an attractive enough term. Although during the economic forum in Krynica Prime Minister Morawiecki has already announced the idea of creating a single big investment zone from Poland, the question is whether it is indeed a government programme supported by concrete legislative proposals and whether there have already been discussions with the European Commission on this matter, because it is clear that the European Commission is concerned about drastic changes in the geography of public aid. The local zone critics claim that they only serve foreign capital, which Poland unnecessarily helps at the expense of our entrepreneurs. Let me stress that these are allegations of heightenedness. Polish companies have second place in terms of invested capital - 20% of all investors. However, the important question remains: How to attract Polish business, especially small and medium? How do we make our capital dominant in special economic zones? Another issue - many places in the report are devoted to the activities of zone clusters. But it's a continuation, not a new value. It is very good that this activity is still being carried out, but it does not prove the creativity of the current boards, because the clusters were founded by the predecessors. Clusters are a form of cooperation between companies already operating and forming a network of cooperation, not top-down imposed organisational structures. Clusters form spontaneously, e.g. co-operation network around Toyota Wałbrzych. There is no doubt that they are one of the drivers of the development of cooperation between companies in special economic zones with companies and institutions operating also outside the zones. Doubts, however... [[♪ The bell ♪]] Can I have another minute? A few seconds? ...the efficiency of cluster activities. They could be more effective. But the very idea of combining entrepreneurs around common sectoral goals, creating a level of cooperation, initiating and supporting continuity of activities on the business line - teaching institutions, i.e. vocational schools, universities, and special economic zones, i.e. educational clusters, is absolutely right. The promotion of vocational education, especially at a time when very many companies are struggling with staff problems by supporting the employment of foreigners, is essential. There are graduates of universities, especially humanities, who have problems finding work in the profession, so smart vocational training could open up good prospects for young people to work, career development, etc. To conclude, I'm going to say that judging zones isn't easy. Their work is based on the activities of many entities, not only the management of zones, but also local governments, voivodships, Polish Investment and Trade Agency. Only the cooperation of these many entities can bring foreign capital to Poland and strengthen the pro-investment of national companies. In several countries there has been a thread whether a special economic zone is needed at all, as far as it is good for the economy. I will remind you that there are such analyses, one of 2015 prepared for the needs of the then Ministry of Economy by a team of scientists from the School of Economics, which indicates many criteria according to which the special zone is not the best. Also, the 2014 NIK audit report, if I recall correctly, was not the most favourable report for special economic zones. When we started working, I remember the first studio approach in the Ministry I prepared the development by examining what the map of investments made today in the zones looks like. Let us remember that one of the positives mentioned here by one of the Members, by Mr Kasprzak, was the reduction of unemployment and the influx of capital in the situation of Poland, which is almost in bankruptcy. This was also mentioned by the honourable Member. These were indeed the motivations of the time, and these motivations are today, as well as the issue of reducing this imbalance between Western and Eastern Poland. If we look at today after almost 20 years of functioning of special economic zones, we will see that the highest investment density is in south-western Poland, thus in an area where neither the degree of unemployment nor capital dysfunction was so high. So we can say after these 20 years that the zones have actually led to the bringing to Poland of many investments, to the bringing of capital, to the creation of jobs, while they certainly did not reduce inequalities between Eastern and Western Poland, as they intended. So those who come to these places, I don't discuss at this point how... You are well aware of how many local government officials have benefited from the issue of bringing the land into the zone just to earn another election campaign - to earn, in public speaking, another election campaign - without even considering whether there is an investment on the horizon, whether it is worth pursuing or not. This is therefore the timetable for the work on the new law. The principles of this law were presented by the Prime Minister in Krynica, today also at the meeting. I mentioned this many times, as I said, it was one of the first things I did at the Ministry of Development. In addition to showing the map of investments carried out today in special zones and these disparities, we analysed simultaneously and tried to answer the question whether a special zone is really needed in Poland or not. And we can say, as many entrepreneurs also say, that special zones - like special exclusions at all - are spoiling, disrupting the market. And this is probably a model answer, but if we look at the Czech Republic, Slovakia Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, and therefore all neighbouring countries, each of them uses a zone element and zones in these countries in different ways. We, when preparing our legislative proposal, took into account all these solutions, also the fact that Lithuania extended its zone privileges to 2030 last year. The model, which seemed to be the most interesting for us from the perspective of attracting investment, was a combined model: Czech-Slovak, where there is no territorial designation, the marking of the zone, the areas to which it is possible to apply in time, but these places are set dynamically, taking into account the level of unemployment, infrastructure and the moment of development. Consequently, these countries can almost entirely say of themselves that they are a special economic zone. So this model adopted in the work was similar to the Czech and Slovak model. I'm going to answer some questions. A final remark on the size of the share of investments. And then in areas where there are no such large integrators, e.g. in the vicinity of Białystok, and thus in the area of the Suwałki Special Economic Zone, such a cluster is created by the zone, is the result of the endogenous activity of the zone board. That is why we have highlighted this in this report in a special way, because these clusters have actually become blushing in the last year. To such an extent that, for example, The presence of many small entrepreneurs at events such as Hannover Messe, trade fairs, becomes more visible by the fact that entrepreneurs appear within clusters rather than individually. The pain, the weakness of Polish industry and Polish entrepreneurs in general is what I often repeat and what we also talked about in committee meetings on various occasions - a certain inability to cooperate. In view of the above, the completeness of the records kept by the Minister depends to a large extent on the reliability of the obligation to send the documents indicated to the Minister by notaries and courts. The acquisition of real estate by foreigners requires, in principle, the permission of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. Foreigners are also obliged to obtain permission to acquire or acquire shares or shares in a company, as well as any other action concerning shares or shares of a trading company established in Poland which is the owner or user of perpetual real estate in the territory of Poland. The provisions of the Act are aligned with the requirements of European law, and in particular European freedoms for citizens and entrepreneurs of States Parties to the Agreement on the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation. In 2016, a 12-year transitional period was concluded limiting the purchase of agricultural and forestry property to citizens and businesses originating in the EEA and the Swiss Confederation. From 1 May 2016, they may acquire real estate under the same conditions as Polish citizens without the need to apply for the permission of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. As in previous years, there is a small number of permissions granted for the acquisition, in principle taking up shares or shares in companies owned by or users of perpetual real estate located in Poland, because capital turnover for entities originating in the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation is of course free and there are no restrictions in this respect. The largest number of permits were granted to Ukrainian citizens - 59, Belarus - 11, Russia - 8. The largest area was in Pomeranian, Lower Silesian and West Pomeranian voivodships. 8 permissions to acquire shares and shares in companies owned or owned by perpetual properties in Poland with an area of almost 36.5 ha and owners of commercial premises with a total area of over 326 m2. Here you can say: individuals - Ukrainians, Israeli citizens dominated among their nationalities. This is also from the United States, Mauritius, Hong Kong and Turkey. The properties were mainly located in the Lower Silesian, Lubuskie and Lesser Poland voivodship. In general, the permits concerned the most common properties located in the Dolnośląskie, Mazowieckie and Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship in the scope of all discussed. Among the authorised entities, Ukraine and Belarus dominated, as was apparent from citizenship or capital. In 2016, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs issued 35 decisions refusing foreign nationals to grant permission to acquire land, premises, shares and shares. Within this number, negative decisions were issued because of the foreigner's failure to show his permanent ties with Poland or the failure to comply with statutory conditions justifying the issue of the permit. Within the indicated number of all negative decisions, 10 negative decisions were issued because of the Minister's opposition to agriculture and rural development. The Minister of Home Affairs and Administration shall also undertake activities within the framework of his statutory powers to examine the legality of the acquisition by foreigners of real estate, shares or shares. Where a transaction is found to have been concluded in accordance with the provisions of the Law on the acquisition of immovable property by aliens, an entry in the register of immovable property acquired by aliens shall be made with or without the obligation to obtain such authorisation respectively. In 2016, the number of recorded transactions concerning the acquisition of all land by foreigners increased by 22% compared to the previous year. As in recent years, most buyers have been citizens and entrepreneurs from the European Economic Area. Here I would like to quote for better information that in terms of the area the most land property was acquired by foreigners representing citizenship or capital: German - 881 ha, Dutch - almost 626 ha and Cyprus - almost 330 ha. Housing and utility premises with a total area of 649 740 m2 based on 6395 transactions. And here, when it comes to citizenship or capital nationality, we have Dutch, Ukrainians and Germans. Generally, in 2016, as regards all these transactions, foreigners acquired the most properties in the Mazowieckie, Silesian and Lower Silesian voivodship, and citizenship or capital of the largest number concerned the Germans and the Netherlands. Given that the authorisation is valid 2 years from its date of issue, and only the parties to the transaction depend on when and whether the contract will be concluded, data on the number of authorisations issued in a given calendar year often do not coincide with the data on the real estate acquisition transactions made on the basis of that authorisation. In addition, not every permit means the actual acquisition of the property by the foreigner - you know, the parties to the transaction may waive the intention to conclude the contract despite the consent received. In accordance with the Article. 6 Act to acquire property by a foreigner contrary to the provisions of the Act is invalid. The annulment of the acquisition of the property by the foreigner shall be decided by the court on request, inter alia. Minister for Home Affairs and Administration. This right also has the right due to the location of the estate of the governor, the governor and the Marshal of the voivodship. In 2016, 72 transactions entered into in breach of the law were recorded. Among these cases, in 56 cases, as a result of the actions taken by the Minister responsible for the internal affairs of the parties to the transaction, they themselves led to a state in accordance with the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners, while in the remaining 16 cases the Minister referred to the court for annulment of the acquisition of immovable property by the foreigner by law. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, it should be noted that the registers kept by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration enable the monitoring of trends in the acquisition of real estate, shares and shares by foreigners, an analysis of the scale of interest of individual groups of entities, including individuals, entrepreneurs representing, foreign capital, and the acquisition of specific types of real estate and the degree of interest of such real estate in individual voivodships. The registers kept are a database containing in particular information concerning the property acquired by foreigners and the data of the companies which foreign nationals acquired or acquired their shares. These data are derived from notarial acts sent and court orders, and are obtained in the course of investigations carried out by the authorities. It is therefore very important to ensure that documents relating to the acquisition by foreigners of real estate, shares or shares are fairly transmitted by courts and notaries so that the records kept by the Minister contain complete data in this regard. It also requires that, despite the end of the transitional period during which the permission to acquire agricultural and forestry property by foreigners from the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation was required, the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration is still a body empowered to monitor the trading of immovable property involving foreigners by keeping a register of real estate, shares and shares acquired and covered by aliens. It should be indicated that on 1 January 2017 the Law of 2 December 2016 amending the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and certain other laws entered into force. The changes made are primarily intended to improve the system of obtaining documents and information necessary for the full registration of immovable property, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens. They have extended both the circle of entities obliged to inform the Minister responsible for Home Affairs about events subject to the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners as well as the scope of the documents transmitted. The acquisition of information on specific legal events from 1 January 2017 is carried out from various sources. This includes notaries, courts and public administrations. It is also assumed that this will allow for a faster response in the event of a breach of the law, which will positively affect the certainty of economic turnover. A more complete collection of information will result in the data entered in the above mentioned. registers will accurately reflect the real interest of foreigners in real estate located in Poland and their participation in the Polish capital market. The key changes made by this amendment to the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners are, as I mentioned, the extension of the catalogue sent by notaries and by the courts of documents and the inclusion of all documents resulting from notarial activities and all documents submitted to the register files resulting in the acquisition of real estate, shares and shares, as well as the obligation of the public authorities to send the minister final administrative decisions by virtue of which the foreigner acquired the property. The effectiveness of the changes made to the Real Estate Purchase Act will, of course, only be possible after the full annual period following the entry into force of the amendment. This issue will be examined next year as part of the implementation report of the Foreign Property Purchase Act in 2017. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u412	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It is probably not hard to know that my speech will be in a similar spirit as the speech of the Minister and the rapporteur. Nevertheless, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I submit to the High House an opinion on the report by the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration on the implementation in 2016 of the Law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. High Izbo! The Act of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners is a legal act regulating the acquisition of real estate by foreigners in Poland, because it introduces the general principle that the acquisition of real estate by a foreigner requires authorisation, which is issued by administrative decision by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. This law also regulates the acquisition by foreigners of shares or shares in commercial companies established in the territory of the Republic of Poland which are the owners or users of perpetual real estate. The legal acts governing the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners are the above mentioned law on the acquisition of immovable property, as well as two implementing acts. The first is the Regulation of the Minister of the Interior of 2012 on detailed information and the types of documents to be submitted by the foreigner seeking permission to acquire real estate and the second regulation of the Council of Ministers of 2004 on the procedure and detailed rules for keeping records of real estate, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens. The law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners applies only to entities having the status of aliens. A foreigner within the meaning of Article. 1 paragraph 2 of the Act is a natural person who does not have Polish citizenship, a legal person established abroad, who has no legal personality, a company of persons listed in points 1 or 2, established abroad, established in accordance with the laws of foreign countries, a legal person and a commercial company not having legal personality established in the territory of the Republic of Poland, controlled directly or indirectly by persons or companies listed in points 1, 2 and 3. In the case of a commercial company, a controlled company, within the meaning of the Act, shall be deemed to have a foreigner or foreigners directly or indirectly owned above. 50% votes at a meeting of shareholders or at a general meeting. In view of the above, the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners will not apply, inter alia. to a natural person who has also Polish citizenship alongside the nationality of a foreign State, as well as to a capital company established in the territory of the Republic in which foreigners, partners or shareholders do not have more than 50% of the shares votes at the shareholders' meeting or do not have a dominant position in the company. High Izbo! The Minister of Home Affairs and Administration presented a detailed report on the implementation of the Law in 2016. This report includes, inter alia, issues such as the conditions for the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, the scale of interest, the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, the granting of permits, the examination of the legality of the acquisition of such immovable property and the most important and in-depth statistics needed for this law. Foreigners who were granted permits mostly represented citizenship and capital: Ukrainian - 109 permits, Belarusian - 33 permits, Russian - 16 permits, Armenian - 10 permits and Chinese - 10 permits. The registers of real estate, shares and shares acquired and acquired by foreigners by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration and without the obligation to obtain authorisation should permit monitoring of trends in this area and analysis of the scale of interest of individual groups, entities, natural persons and entrepreneurs representing foreign capital in acquiring specific types of real estate, as well as the degree of interest in such real estate in individual voivodships. The registers kept are a database containing in particular information concerning the property acquired by foreigners and the data of the companies in which foreign nationals acquired or acquired shares. These data are derived from notarial acts sent and court orders, and are obtained in the course of investigations carried out by the Authority. It is also stressed that, despite the end of the transitional period during which the permission to acquire agricultural and forestry property by foreigners from the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation was required, the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration is still a body empowered to monitor the trading of immovable property involving foreigners by keeping a register of real estate, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens. It should be indicated that on 1 January 2017 the Law of 2 December 2016 amending the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and certain other laws entered into force. The changes made are primarily intended to improve the system of obtaining documents and information necessary for the full registration of immovable property, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-14-sejm-47-3.u426	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present an opinion on the report by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on the implementation in 2016 of the Law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. In 2016, the Government of Law and Justice introduced restrictions on the sale of agricultural land in the Republic, restricting the possibility of selling agricultural land within the administrative borders of cities which, as has been repeatedly stressed and alarmed, will not have an essential impact on the purchase of Polish land by foreigners, but will, in a drastic way, prevent Polish citizens from trading private agricultural land in terms of both their sale and purchase. Today in the Sejm we are discussing a report prepared by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration concerning, among others. this issue and how the acquisition of land by foreigners developed in 2016. The Minister's report states that in 2016 a total of 486 applications for permission to acquire real estate and shares in companies owned or used by perpetual real estate located in Poland were received. This number covered 478 real estate applications and the remaining 8 concerned shares and shares. The Ministry issued e.g. 356 permits and 35 refusals. In 2016, foreigners received a total of 251 permits for the acquisition of land with a total area of 79.81 ha: legal persons received 18 permits for the total area of 47,70 ha of real estate, natural persons received 233 permits for the acquisition of real estate with a total area of 32,10 ha. Legal persons were mainly interested in obtaining permission to acquire real estate located in voivodships: Dolnośląskie (16.30 ha), Wielkopolskie (6.41 ha), Kujawsko-Pomorskie (12.56 ha), Lubelskie (4.84 ha). Natural persons were mainly interested in obtaining permission to acquire properties located in the voivodships: Mazowieckie (14.04 ha), Wielkopolskie (2.48 ha), Małopolska (3.43 ha) and Dolnośląskie (2.32 ha). Among foreigners authorised (natural persons and legal persons combined), Ukrainian citizenship and capital (109 permits), Belarusian (33 permits), Russian (16 permits), Armenian (10 permits), Chinese (10 permits). The Minister's report also states that, after a comparison of data from the last 5 years, it is concluded that both the number of permits issued for the acquisition of land by foreigners and the area of the authorised property in 2016 decreased significantly compared to previous years (as compared to 2015 the number of permits decreased by 25%, while the area of the premises covered by the permits decreased by 83%. ). This results in the end of the transitional period concerning restrictions on the acquisition of agricultural and forestry property by citizens and entrepreneurs from the European Economic Area. According to the reports, 318 permits were issued in 2012 and 252 permits were issued in 2013 and 271 permits in 2014 and almost identical to those in 2015 and 251 permits in 2016 (one less than in 2013). In view of these figures, no substantial difference was recorded in terms of the overall low volume of licences issued in 2016. The area was slightly larger: in 2012 and 2014 it amounted to just over 100 ha, in 2013 and 697 ha, in 2015 and 460 ha in 2016, and only 80 ha in 2016. It also reduced the number of permits for the acquisition of agricultural and forestry property (2012 - 218 permits, 2013 - 166, 2014 - 166, 2015 - 162, 2016 - 51 permits), but increased the number of permits for residential and commercial premises (2012 - 80 permits, 2013 - 57, 2014 - 70, 2015 - 68, 2016 - 96 permits respectively). Statistics on the subject could be quoted for a long time, but after the above mentioned, it can be seen that the problem of buying land and property in Poland in general does not exist. These transactions represent only a small percentage of all such transactions. This raises the question again: Are we even dealing with the problem of buying Polish land by foreigners in Poland? Neither the figures nor the figures from recent years confirm this trend. This raises another question: Is it appropriate to introduce draconian restrictions where there is no problem, in addition in a way that leaves the various possibilities for foreign parties to circumvent the new law, while in a fundamental way it strikes Polish citizens and their freedom to own them? The answer to both questions seems obvious: no. And with this conclusion, we remain, among others, established in it. the report by the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration referred to here.

Jerzy Popieluszko was born on 14 September 1947 in the village of Okopy, near Suchowola, where he graduated from primary school and high school. Jerzy Popieluszka went to study in Rome, but the priest refused. On 19 October 1984, he arrived at the parish of St. Holy Polish Martyr Brothers in Bydgoszcz, where he celebrated Mass of St. for people who work. Returning to Warsaw together with his driver Waldemar Chrostowski, he was abducted by the officers of the independent Group of the Department IV of the Ministry of Internal Affairs dealing with the fight against the Catholic Church. The drivers managed to escape, and the priest was bound and held in the trunk. On October 30th, his body was fished from the Vistula reservoir near Włocławek. The priest's hands were bound, and the body was loaded with a sack full of stones. The autopsy showed signs of torture. Killing and revealing the truth about Fr. Imperial shock and social outrage. The funeral gathered huge crowds and turned into a great manifestation. 13 October 2009 in connection with the 25th anniversary of the death of Fr. Jerzy Popiełuszko was posthumously decorated by the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński as the Order of the White Eagle. In 2001 the beatification process ended on 6 June 2010 in Warsaw, where the Mass was held, presided over by Archbishop Angelo Amato. The Archbishop, on behalf of Pope Benedict XVI, read the beatification act. Martyrdom Fr. Jerzy Popieluszko is a loud cry of love confirmed by the price of bloodshed. Thank you very much. Created since 1937, on the initiative of Minister Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski, the Central Industrial District was intended to increase the economic potential of the country, develop heavy and armed industries and reduce unemployment caused by the effects of the great crisis from the 20s. 20th century In the centre of Poland there were established large centres of heavy, machine and chemical industries. This year we celebrate the 80th anniversary of the beginnings of this so-called. an economic miracle. The location of COP between Warsaw, Cracow and Lviv was due to many factors. COP covered areas of former voivodships: Kielce, Tarnobrzeg, Tarnowski, Rzeszowski and partly Radom, Kraków and Lublin, in total - approx. 60 thousand. km2. This area was inhabited by over 5600 thousand. people, of which more than 80% They were inhabitants of poor, overcrowded villages. COP's investments were accompanied by the development of communication and energy infrastructure, among others. in sandy, forest-covered areas separated from several villages, modern plants were built, steel factory and chemical plants in Nowa Sarzyna, which, together with their residential estates, completely changed the lives of thousands of people. In early September this year. the central celebration of the 80th anniversary of the COP was held in Stalowa Wola. During this ceremony, the town and municipality of Nowa Sarzyna presented their achievements, the city of which the entire post-war development was closely connected with local chemical plants. Joint investments of municipalities and chemical plants in the fields of culture, science and sport have permanently entered the landscape. After changes in the factory's structure, many companies were created, such as Ciech Sarzyna, Polenergia Elektrociepłownia Nowa Sarzyna, Zakład Chemiczny Silicony Polskie, Wod Rem, Chemrem, Budorg, NS Automation, El-Chem and others, still cooperate with local government on many levels of economic and social life. High Izbo! I appeal to the High House to give a witness not only on the occasion of the following anniversaries, of the importance of this biggest investment project of the Second Republic. I hope that the government will continue to build the economic power of Poland on plants created within the Central Industrial District. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
